# Phase 13 — Persian, and images

Two requests from the maintainer on 2026-10-01: "add Persian professionally,
right now" (D-175), and images across the product after a review of the
options, of which they chose crests and competition logos, licensed news
photos and player photos (path "A + B + C"; D-176, D-177).

**Where Persian starts.** The locale machinery exists (T-150, T-300, T-302):
routing, direction, plural rules from CLDR, a catalogue per locale, a
fallback that says so. 563 strings go through the catalogue; most of a
page's other text is English written in the page itself, which a catalogue
cannot translate. `Intl` gives Persian the Solar Hijri calendar and Persian
digits without any code (`۹ مهر ۱۴۰۵، ۲۲:۰۰`, `۶۴٪`).

**Where images start.** API-Football's responses carry crest, logo, flag and
photo URLs that no adapter keeps; news feeds carry enclosures that the
parser drops; D-061 took no news images. A reader's browser must never
request a third party (the D-089 precedent), and no provider URL may reach
the API (rule 2), so every image is fetched once by the server, stored, and
served from the site's own origin.

**Done on 2026-10-01.** T-1320 (#490) and T-1321 (#492): 270 club crests and
the competitions' logos were fetched within the first hour and are served
from `/api/media/...`; a team without one shows a lettered mark. T-1322
(#491, #495): photos from Mehr, Tasnim and Tehran Times are decided, stored
and carried on the story contracts with their credit; the maintainer chose to
accept a photo whose page names no other agency (D-177). T-1323 draws them (#498); the `/media/news/*` route reached readers only once caddy was recreated, which `rollout.sh` now does when its config changes (#501). Flags are not shown:
no decision covers a flag package.

during FIFA windows: the UEFA Nations League, international friendlies, the
Asian Cup and the Africa Cup of Nations qualification (D-179).

## Backups

The maintainer asked on 2026-10-01 whether everything is backed up and for a
complete copy on their own computer, on an external drive (`E:\Backup`). The
nightly dump went off-provider every night and the monthly drill passed; the
media volume (T-1320, T-1322) was in no backup.

| ID | Task | Deps | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[x]` T-1341 | The media volume in the nightly backup (`<remote>/media/`, copied not synced; the remote's pruning reads the top level only), and `pull-copy.sh` for a dated copy on the maintainer's machine: dump and manifest (sha256 checked), media as one `media.tar`, a bundle of the repository, `INFO.txt`; secrets never copied | T-072, T-1320 | The next nightly run records the media count in `backup_run`; `pull-copy.sh /e/Backup` writes a copy whose dump matches its manifest and whose bundle verifies |
| `[x]` T-1344 | `rollout.sh` prunes BuildKit cache older than `ROLLOUT_BUILD_CACHE_HOURS` (default 72) after each rollout: the cache had reached 57 GB of the 75 GB disk (85 % used; 46 % after a manual prune on 2026-10-01) | T-074 | A rollout ends with the prune line in its log; `docker system df` shows no build cache older than three days |
| `[x]` T-1350 | A match a day or more ahead is never shown as played: the API-Football adapter drops a fixture it is told is live or finished when its kick-off is at least 24 hours after the answer arrived, as it drops an unknown status. On production the provider listed AFCON qualifier 1545957 (Tunisia v Botswana, 28 March 2027) as "FT" 2-2, and group H counted it | T-1333 | `api-football.spec.ts`: "FT" and "1H" a day ahead give no fixture; "NS" and "PST" a day ahead and "FT" within the day are kept |
| `[x]` T-1374 | Less WAL (D-192): the production database (918 MB) archived about 433 MB of gzipped WAL a day, which held point-in-time recovery to 3 days in the 10 GB remote (D-157 amended 2026-10-08). Measured read-only on production (`pg_stat_user_tables` and `pg_stat_wal` deltas, `pg_waldump` of the archived segments, the remote's `wal/` listing): about 90% of the WAL bytes were full-page images, and the largest sources were rows rewritten or locked though nothing changed. (1) `findMapping` moves `provider_mapping.last_seen_at` at most once a day per mapping (`MAPPING_SEEN_RESOLUTION_SECONDS`), not on every resolution (about 205,000 row versions a day for 8,500 mappings). (2) The data-quality upsert leaves an unchanged finding out before `ON CONFLICT`, which locked all open findings every five minutes even when its `WHERE` was false. (3) The same for every per-row upsert of the ingestion writers (participants, scores, periods, incidents, line-ups, team and player statistics, absences) and `entity_media`: `INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS` the identical row, the conflict clause kept for a concurrent writer. (4) Postgres `checkpoint_timeout` 15 min, `max_wal_size` 2 GB, `wal_compression` lz4, applied by an operator's `docker compose up -d postgres` (`09-deploy.md` §9). (5) `pitr.sh measure` usable with archiving on: the gzip ratio from the archive, the WAL and bases already archived not counted twice, `--days N` for a longer window | T-845, T-026 | `- [x]` `ingestion.spec.ts`: a mapping last seen 25 hours ago is moved on (new `xmin`), then three resolutions leave the same row version. `- [x]` `data-quality.http.spec.ts`: a sweep within the hour leaves every finding's `xmin` and `xmax` as they were; after an hour `last_seen_at` still moves on. `- [x]` `ingestion-jobs.spec.ts`: a replayed post-match ask leaves all 79 rows the recorded match wrote (fixture, participants, scores, periods, incidents, statistics, line-up, player statistics) at the same `xmin`/`xmax`; one value changed in each of six tables is put back (6 written, those rows new versions, every other row untouched). `- [x]` `media.http.spec.ts`: noting a held address returns false at the same row version; a new address rewrites the row and makes it pending. `- [x]` Each of these fails against the previous code. `- [x]` D-192 with the measurements, the expected saving per change and the path back to a 7-day window; `07-backups.md`, `09-deploy.md` and the project map updated. Nothing deployed and no Postgres setting changed on production by this task |

## The model, with a collaborator

The maintainer's teammate asked on 2026-10-04 to work on the forecast model
with us: tested on completed matches, every forecast stored and compared with
its result, accuracy over time, the formula and the weights tunable from real
performance. The storage and the evaluation existed; what was missing was one
documented path and one command. Accuracy over time and RPS in the API and
the web are T-1369's.

## The Persian Gulf Pro League's constants

A read-only investigation on 2026-10-08 found IR1, the one league the model
reads from our own records, fitted with the published decay and ridge by
both the published version and 0.5.0: a 55.8% draw for Tractor v Esteghlal,
91.5% for Persepolis over Sanat Naft. No tuning had covered it.

## Forecast freshness

The 2026-10-08 investigation: an `early` forecast is written once, seven days
before kick-off, and the next only when a line-up is stored, about an hour
before it, so for most of a week the page showed a forecast fitted before
results played since (the previous round, a midweek match).

a result of today, one the fit already read, a cup result for a league fixture, a result with no full-time score and an unavailable version move nothing (`forecast-triggers.spec.ts`). `- [x]` The limits: nothing until the newest version is 24 hours old, with its own skip reason; ## Page speed (GTmetrix, 2026-10-21)

GTmetrix graded the homepage E (performance 40 %, LCP 5.4 s, TTFB 4.1 s). The
plan, in Persian, with every finding checked against production:
https://claude.ai/artifact/6WSnQXeVwYx1t62hdAAiAh. Phase 1 is T-1450,
T-1451 and T-1456; T-1452 (a Redis cache of the public reads) waits for the
re-run, because T-1450 brought those reads to tens of milliseconds. The
maintainer's Cloudflare steps are in the plan.

| ID | Task | Deps | Acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `[x]` T-1450 | News reads build cards only for the stories a section can list. Every section built the card -- the shown article, its first publication, the other reports -- for all ~15,700 stories, then filtered and sorted: `/news` took 2.1-2.5 s and `/news/breaking` 1.4-1.9 s with nothing marked, measured one at a time on production, and the homepage waited on both. `Query.pick` adds conditions every listable story meets (breaking: a live mark; debate: a selection; trending: a save or a discussed match in the window; following, a player's, a match's and the team, competition and type filters: the linked stories; the match's window and the `from` date: a report fetched or published since), read first in a `MATERIALIZED` CTE so the planner starts from them. The unfiltered latest section reads through `NEWEST_WINDOWS` (3 hours, 1 day, 7 days, unbounded): a window that fills the rows a page reads holds exactly the unbounded read's rows. The WHERE that decides is unchanged. EXPLAIN ANALYZE on production of the new SQL: latest 36 ms (was 2,056), breaking 0.3 ms, a competition's news 75 ms (was 1,238), debate and trending under 1 ms | -- | `- [x]` `news-read-windows.http.spec.ts`: the windowed latest equals the unbounded read for page sizes 1, 2, 5 and 30, from eight cursors, for a reader and for none, in one snapshot; this run's stories are all listed past every window. `- [x]` The 29 news specs (166 tests) on a migrated, seeded scratch database; typecheck and lint |

On 2026-10-21 ClaudeBot alone sent 2,134 of 2,534 origin requests in thirty minutes (about seventy a minute; the delay allows six) | -- | `- [x]` `demonstration.spec.ts`: the throttled group, its delay and its disallow list equal to everyone's; no group beside `Disallow: /` on demonstration data. `- [x]` `tests/e2e/seo.spec.ts`: the served robots.txt carries ClaudeBot's delay. `- [x]` Typecheck and lint |

The same helpers: 88 ms | -- | `- [x]` `intl-cache.spec.ts`: the same object for the same locale and options, a different one otherwise, formatting as `Intl` does. `- [x]` The web unit suite (173 files, 2,014 tests, `format.spec.ts` included), typecheck, lint, and the first-load JavaScript within every budget |
| `[x]` T-1467 | Guests' copies of the busiest pages are kept at Cloudflare's edge for 30 seconds (D-244), the maintainer's choice of 2026-10-22 after phase 3 of the GTmetrix plan. The free plan's cache rules cannot set an edge TTL under two hours, so the origin says it: Caddy answers a plain GET of /en, /fa, their scores, match and competition pages `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=30` when the request carries no `fmip_*` cookie (session, first run, theme, text size, contrast, motion, lite, slow, held notice), no `Save-Data: on` and no Next router header; everything else keeps the web app's `private, no-store`. The Cloudflare cache rule "guest pages" (same paths, no `fmip_` in the Cookie header, no Save-Data) is eligible for cache with the edge TTL taken from the origin. Acceptance: on production a guest's second request for /en within 30 s is `cf-cache-status: HIT`, and the same request with a `fmip_theme` cookie, with `Save-Data: on` or with `RSC: 1` is not served from the cache. Measured on 2026-10-22: guest /en, /fa and /en/scores MISS then HIT at 0.08–0.10 s; with `fmip_theme` or Save-Data `DYNAMIC`; `RSC` `BYPASS`. |
| `[x]` T-1468 | A run a deploy interrupts no longer holds its job for two hours (D-245). Found on production on 2026-10-22: the rollout at 07:0x UTC stopped the old API container mid-way through a `post_match` batch, the run stayed `running`, and every later tick refused to start until the two-hour stale reclaim at 09:00 -- four post-match runs lost (800 backlog matches), and a just-finished match's full-time would have waited the same. `IngestRunsService` now remembers the runs it opened and, on SIGTERM (`beforeApplicationShutdown`), closes those still open as failed with `interrupted: the process running it was stopped`; the pool drain moves to `onApplicationShutdown` so that write can still happen. Acceptance: the integration spec opens one run and finishes another in this process beside a third process's open run; after the shutdown hook only the first is closed, and the job can be opened again at once. |
| `[x]` T-1469 | No stylesheet holds back the first paint, and the homepage's one vague link names where it goes (D-246). PageSpeed on production on 2026-10-22 (mobile /en: performance 84, SEO 92) put two CSS requests (1.8 + 12.8 kB) on the critical path at an estimated 1.8 s, and flagged the first-run offer's link "Start". `next.config.ts` turns on `experimental.inlineCss`, so the page's CSS arrives inside the HTML; the offer's link now reads the first run's own title ("Make FMIP yours", already translated), and `firstRun.offer.start` is gone from every catalogue. Acceptance: a production build's `/en` has one inline `<style>` and no `<link rel="stylesheet">`; the i18n call-site check passes; PageSpeed's render-blocking and link-text findings are gone after the deploy. |
| `[x]` T-1470 | A slow connection found after load no longer redraws the page (D-247). PageSpeed desktop on 2026-10-22 measured `/en` CLS 0.282 on the main column. Reproduced with Playwright as a guest at 1350x940: on a fast connection CLS is 0.002; with the connection reported as 3G, `LiteProvider` turned lite mode on after hydration -- the banner pushed in (45 px), the live strip compacted, the fonts and lists redrawn -- for a shift of 0.10 every load. By then the HTML had already asked for its images and fonts (the CSS is inline since T-1469), so the late switch saved almost nothing. `LiteProvider` now renders the server's mode only; what the browser reports goes to the hint cookie `fmip_slow`, and the next page is lite from the server's first byte with its banner in the HTML. Acceptance: the spec holds that lite mode is derived from the server's answer alone and that the hint is still written; a guest on 3G gets no layout shift from lite mode on the first page and a lite second page. |
| `[x]` T-1471 | A provider request that stalls fails after 30 seconds (D-248). On production on 2026-10-22 the 11:00 UTC `post_match` run fetched 172 matches by 11:02 and then sat open for over half an hour with no query running: the live transport (`TimedTransport`) had no limit of its own, and Node's are five minutes for the headers and five more for the body, so a provider that stalled held the batch -- and the job's lock -- one request after another. Each request now carries `AbortSignal.timeout(30 s)` for the whole exchange; an aborted request is a `transport` failure the adapter already reports. Acceptance: a stalled fetch rejects within the bound, an answer in time passes through parsed, and the default is 30 s (`timed-transport.spec.ts`). |
| `[x]` T-1472 | A run's outcome survives a database restart (D-249). Two deploys on 2026-10-22 still left runs open after T-1468. The captured log of the stopped container showed why: the rollout recreated Postgres at 12:00:43 (its archive setting differs from `.env` while archiving is paused), the jobs in flight failed on their connections, and the write of that failure failed too -- "the database system is shutting down" -- after `finish` had already dropped the run from the open set, so the shutdown hook had nothing to close. A one-off API process on production, sent a real SIGTERM, closed its open run as designed. `track` now writes the outcome once, outside the work's `try`, through `finishSurely`, retried at 1, 2, 4, 8 and 15 s; a run leaves the open set only once written. `deploy/rollout.sh` migrates with `--no-deps`, so a rollout no longer recreates Postgres at all. Acceptance: with the store's `finish` failing twice the run is written `succeeded` on the third try; failing always, the job rejects after five retries and the shutdown hook still closes the run. |
| `[x]` T-1473 | Three faults from the production audit of 2026-10-22 (D-250). (1) The provider's per-minute limit: `standings` asks every competition within three seconds, and right after a paced bulk job the provider refused part of it ("rateLimit") -- 10 standings and 7 fixtures runs in 24 hours left partial, a table an hour stale each time. The API-Football adapter now asks again after 2 s and then 5 s on a per-minute refusal (`rateLimit` in the envelope), and never on the day's quota or our own budget's 429. (2) `people_names` asked the player-profile endpoint for coaches adopted from line-ups (`coach:21702`: "The Player field must contain an integer"), because its exclusion looked only for a coach's matches and spells; it now also skips any `coach:` id. (3) `duplicate_fixture` held 50 open findings, every one two separate matches -- national teams' friendly double-headers and MLS 2020 -- each with its own id, round and result; two finished fixtures at least 24 hours apart are no longer a duplicate. (4) The watchdog's `data_quality` condition counted findings on "a match live or kicked off in the last six hours" as `kickoff_at >= now - 6 h`, so every future fixture qualified, and a double-header friendly set for 15 November held it at `degraded` from 21 October; the window now ends at now. Acceptance: `rate-limit.spec.ts` (retried and answered; given up after its pauses; the day's quota not retried), the people-names store spec (a coach known by his id alone is never owed), the data-quality spec (a played double-header is quiet, a same-day pair still found), and the data-quality integration spec (a finding counts once its match has kicked off, not before). |
| `[x]` T-1474 | A coach's id is never sent to the player-profile endpoint, by any job (D-250 point 2). After T-1473 was deployed, the 16:24 UTC `people_names` run still asked about `coach:9500` and `coach:25857` ("Nico Estevez", a full name the name sweep never selects): the same run's bio pass (`person-bio-store.ts`, T-1428) excluded only coaches with a match, not by id nor by spell. Its query now skips `coach:` ids and coaching spells, and the API-Football adapter's `getPersonProfile` answers a `coach:` id as `unsupported` without a request, so no caller can send one. Acceptance: the adapter spec (no request for `coach:9500`), and the bio store spec (a coach adopted from a line-up, known by his id alone, is not owed). |
| `[x]` T-1475 | The same match stored with home and away swapped is found (D-251). A data audit on 2026-10-23 found seven friendlies held twice -- the provider's two ids for one match, six of them with the sides reversed and the score mirrored (Pakistan v Kenya, Mauritius v Djibouti, Djibouti v Pakistan, Costa Rica v Ecuador, Puerto Rico v Curaçao, Uganda v Senegal); `duplicate_fixture` paired only fixtures with the same home team, so six went unseen. The pair query now also reads two fixtures of the same season with the sides swapped kicking off within `SWAPPED_WINDOW_HOURS` (3) of each other, never a second leg days apart; the finding names it. Acceptance: the unit spec (a mirrored pair at the same time is found, a reversed second leg six days later is not), and the swapped join run read-only on production returns exactly the six. |
| `[x]` T-1476 | Two settlement runs for one fixture no longer collide (D-252). CI failed on 2026-10-22 with `settlement_one_settled_per_prediction`: two runs over the same finished fixture -- here two specs' passes, in production the scheduler's and an operator's, or two containers in a rollout -- each read a prediction as unsettled and both inserted, and the second run failed whole. `record` now takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the fixture, re-reads the newest settlement of each prediction under it, and writes only what is still owed; the run row and `settleFixture`'s answer carry the counts actually written. The friend-predictions spec, which saw a rare 500 on registration in CI, now prints the response body when it fails, so the next occurrence names its cause. Acceptance: the settlement integration spec runs two `settleFixture` calls at once on one fixture -- no error, one wrote both predictions, the other none, two settled rows. |

On the server: `fmip-wal-ship.timer` disabled, the local spool emptied, `wal/` and `base/` purged from the remote (9.44 → 2.82 GiB). In the repository: `backup.sh` dumps with `--compress=zstd:9` (a third smaller than gzip at 6, measured on `lineup` and `incident`) and keeps the newest remote dumps within `BACKUP_REMOTE_BUDGET_GB` (7), never fewer than `BACKUP_REMOTE_MIN_COPIES` (3), deleting each older dump with its manifest and never leaving a gap; `docs/07-backups.md` says point-in-time recovery is paused and how to bring it back. Acceptance: the budget loop, run against a stub rclone, keeps the newest three over a too-small budget and deletes everything older in one run; the next nightly backup reaches the remote and the watchdog's `backup` condition returns to ok. Verified on 2026-10-25: the 03:30 UTC nightly (`fmip-20261025T033050Z`, 517 MB with zstd against 553 MB with gzip the day before) reached the remote, the budget step kept 28 dumps in 3.66 GB, the remote held 5.26 GiB in all (media 0.88 GiB, and a 0.97 GiB base backup left from a 2026-10-24 run made while archiving was still on), and the watchdog's `backup` condition returned to ok. |

## The Stadiomind redesign

The maintainer was not satisfied with the Hybrid design (2026-10-16) and asked for a complete redesign with a real brand. The brief (2026-10-24): English first, phone and desktop equally, Sofascore and Opta Analyst as references, every page from scratch. The canvas "Stadiomind Redesign" (https://claude.ai/artifact/X5qzAJELjXfdsYcHcMUvAn) shows every page on production data of 2026-10-24; the Stadiomind Design System (https://claude.ai/artifact/XXXSf9yhanxxCLcMtuYyrC) holds the tokens, 13 components and the logo. Decisions: D-262 (the name), D-263 (the visual system), D-264 (rollout and budgets). Each task below is one PR; T-1495 to T-1500 can run in parallel once T-1494 is merged.

| ID | Task | Deps | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
